{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 13.37 0 C 13.37 7.39 7.39 13.37 0 13.37 C -7.39 13.37 -13.37 7.39 -13.37 0 C -13.37 -7.39 -7.39 -13.37 0 -13.37 C 7.39 -13.37 13.37 -7.39 13.37 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.04169pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{63}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{i=N}^{n}\chi_{n=N-1}\chi_{s>2}-\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{k=N-n+1}^{s-1}\hbox to19.73pt{\vbox to19.73pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 9.86504pt\lower-9.86504pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 13.37 0 C 13.37 7.39 7.39 13.37 0 13.37 C -7.39 13.37 -13.37 7.39 -13.37 0 C -13.37 -7.39 -7.39 -13.37 0 -13.37 C 7.39 -13.37 13.37 -7.39 13.37 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.04169pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{63}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{i=N}^{n}\chi_{n\neq N-1}\chi_{s\geq N-n+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N-3}\sum_{k=j-n+1}^{M(n)}(\lambda_{2n+k-N}-\lambda_{2n+k-N+1})\chi_{s\geq N-n+1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N-3}\sum_{k=N-n+1}^{s-1}(\lambda_{n+1}-\lambda_{n+2})\chi_{s\geq N-n+2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{k=2}^{M(N-2)}(\lambda_{N+k-4}-\lambda_{N+k-3})\chi_{s>2}+\sum_{k=3}^{s-1}\lambda_{N-1}\chi_{s>3}+\sum_{k=1}^{M(N-1)}\lambda_{N+k-2}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{k=2}^{s-1}\lambda_{N-1}\chi_{n=N-1}\chi_{s>2}-\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{k=N-n+1}^{s-1}(\lambda_{n}-\lambda_{n+1})\chi_{n\neq N-1}\chi_{s\geq N-n+2}

Subcase IV.a: (
s=2
)

\displaystyle\Delta_{N-1}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{M(N-1)}\lambda_{N+k-2}=\lambda_{N-1}

(B.44)

Subcase IV.b: (
s=3
)

\displaystyle\Delta_{N-1}^{3}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=2}^{M(N-2)}(\lambda_{N+k-4}-\lambda_{N+k-3})+\sum_{k=1}^{M(N-1)}\lambda_{N+k-2}-\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\sum_{k=2}^{s-1}\lambda_{N-1}

(B.45)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{N-2}-\cancel{\lambda_{N-1}}+\cancel{\lambda_{N-1}}-\lambda_{N-1}=\lambda_{N-2,N-1}

Subcase IV.c: (
s=4
)

\displaystyle\Delta_{N-1}^{4}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=3}^{3}(\lambda_{2n+k-n-3}-\lambda_{2n+k-n-2})+\sum_{k=2}^{2}(\lambda_{N+k-4}-\lambda_{N+k-3})

(B.46)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(s-3)\lambda_{N-1}+\lambda_{N-1}-\sum_{k=3}^{3}(\lambda_{N-2}-\lambda_{N-1})-2\lambda_{N-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{N-3}-\lambda_{N-2}+\cancel{\lambda_{N-2}}-\cancel{\lambda_{N-1}}+\cancel{\lambda_{N-1}}+\cancel{\lambda_{N-1}}-\cancel{\lambda_{N-2}}+\cancel{\lambda_{N-1}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\cancel{2 \lambda(N-1)}=\lambda_{N-3,N-2}

Subcase IV.d: (
N\geq s>4
)

\displaystyle\Delta_{j}^{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=N-s+1}^{N-3}(\lambda_{n}-\lambda_{n+1})+\sum_{n=N-s+2}^{N-3}\sum_{k=N-n+1}^{s-1}(\lambda_{n+1}-\lambda_{n+2})+\lambda_{N-2}\cancel{\lambda_{N-1}}

(B.47)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(s-3)\lambda_{N-1}+\cancel{\lambda_{N-1}}-\sum_{n=N-s+2}^{N-2}\sum_{k=N-n+1}^{s-1}(\lambda_{n}-\lambda_{n+1})-(s-2)\lambda_{N-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{N-s+1}-\cancel{\lambda_{N-2}}+\sum_{n=N-s+2}^{N-3}(s-N+n-1)(\lambda_{n+1}-\lambda_{n+2})+\cancel{\lambda_{N-2}}-\lambda_{N-1}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{n=N-s+2}^{N-2}(s-N+n-1)(\lambda_{n}-\lambda_{n+1})=\lambda_{N-s+1}-\lambda_{N-1}+\sum_{n=N-s+2}^{N-3}\lambda_{n+1}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(s-6)\lambda_{N-1}-\sum_{n=N-s+2}^{N-2}\lambda_{n}+(s-5)\lambda_{N-1}=\lambda_{N-s+1}-\cancel{\lambda_{N-1}}+\cancel{\lambda_{N-1}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{n=N-s+3}^{N-2}\lambda_{n}-\sum_{n=N-s+2}^{N-2}\lambda_{n}=\lambda_{N-s+1}-\lambda_{N-s+2}=\lambda_{N-s+1,N-s+2}

Clearly,
\Delta_{j}^{s}
is non-negative in every case, so Condition 1 is satisfied.

Cases III.a, III.b, IV.a and IV.b are, in fact, described by a single formula, e.g. the one from Case III.a. Hence we will merge them to a case called Case III (
N>m>N-s+1
).

B.3
Comparison of
\Delta
and
\delta(m)
- Condition 2

By comparing the values of
\Delta^{k}_{i}
and
\delta^{k}_{i}(m)
in overlapping cases, one may check that indeed
\Delta^{k}_{i}\geq\delta^{k}_{i}(m)
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N-1\}
,
k\in\{2,\ldots,N\}
and
m\in\{1,\ldots,N-1\}
. Hence Condition 2 is satisfied.

Appendix C
Conditions 3 and 4

C.1
Condition 3 (row counting)

Clearly, the partial sum of entries of a telescope
T_{p,q}
of the form (
43
) is a telescopic sum equal

\begin{pmatrix}\lambda_{p+q-1}\\
\lambda_{p+q-2}\\
\vdots\\
\lambda_{p+1}\\
\lambda_{p}\end{pmatrix}\mathrm{,}

(C.1)

from top to bottom and

\begin{pmatrix}\lambda_{p}\\
\lambda_{p}-\lambda_{p+q-1}\\
\vdots\\
\lambda_{p}-\lambda_{p+2}\\
\lambda_{p}-\lambda_{p+1}\end{pmatrix}\mathrm{,}

(C.2)

from bottom to top. Note that the entries of (
C.1
) are determined, up to position
q
, only by the top entry
\lambda_{p+q-1}
, which in turn depends only on
p+q
. Let
\sigma_{n,k}
be the difference between the rows in which top virtual boxes of columns, for two consecutive labels
n
and
n+1
, appear at a given step
k
. Note that from (
45
), for fixed
k
and
m>n
, we have
\text{beg}(m,k)>\text{beg}(n,k)
. Thus,
\sigma_{n,k}
is either
1
or
2
and
\sigma_{n,k}=2
is obtained exactly in step
k=N-n
. Moreover, from (
46
), for fixed
k
and
m>n
, we have
\text{len}(m,k)\geq\text{len}(n,k)
. Hence, the difference
\tau_{n,k}
between lengths of telescopes for two consecutive labels
n
and
n+1
, at given step
k
, is either
0
or
1
. Thus, it suffices to consider three cases, depicted in Fig.
C.1
.

\lambda_{r}

\lambda_{r+1}

\vdots

\vdots

(a)

Case 1:

\sigma_{n,k}=1,\,\tau_{n,k}=0

\lambda_{r}

\lambda_{r}

\vdots

\vdots

(b)

Case 2:

\sigma_{n,k}=2,\,\tau_{n,k}=1

\lambda_{r}

\lambda_{r}

\vdots

\vdots

(c)

Case 3:

\sigma_{n,k}=1,\,\tau_{n,k}=1

Figure C.1
:
Possible relative positions of the columns of virtual boxes corresponding to telescopes (columns on the left – with label
n
, columns on the right – with label
n+1
). Symbols on the top indicate the relationship between the top entries of compared telescopes (i.e. top entries of (
C.1
)).

Since (
C.1
) is non-increasing from top to bottom, it is clear that Condition 3 is satisfied locally (i.e. for any two telescopes with consecutive labels, in any fixed step) in every case, so Condition 3 is always satisfied.

C.2
Condition 4 (column counting)

We show that Condition 4 in fact follows from what we already have proved. Let
\tilde{\#}(m)^{k}_{l}
denote the number of boxes with label
m
appended in step
k
up to the
l
th column (counted from right to left). Notice that the inequalities proved within Condition 2,
\Delta_{i}^{k}\geq\delta_{i}^{k}(m)
, imply that, for a fixed step
k
, the blocks with the same label are distributed stair-like (block in row
i
ends before or just as the block in row
i+1
begins), as shown in Fig.
C.2
.

Figure C.2
:
Stair-like distribution of blocks with label
m
at step
k
. Notice that the spacings might in fact be equal to zero, contrary to what the figure might suggest.

Moreover, in every row the labels of blocks are in non-decreasing order (from left to right). In order to prove that Condition 4 is satisfied, it suffices to show that

\tilde{\#}(m-1)^{k}_{l_{0}}-\tilde{\#}(m)^{k}_{l_{0}}\geq 0\mathrm{,}

(C.3)

whenever it makes sense.
We distinguish two cases - either the column
l_{0}
crosses some
m
-block, say at row
i
, (Case I) or it does not (Case II). We start with Case I.

Case I

There are essentially two subcases presented in Fig.
C.3
. Other possibilities fulfilling of Conditions 1 and 2 can be effectively reduced to those cases. The column in consideration is denoted by
l_{0}
, the columns where an
(m-1)
-block begins and an
m
-block ends are denoted by
l^{\prime}
and
l
respectively.

(a)

Subcase I.A - the
m
-block which intersects with column
l_{0}
at row
i
begins before (or just as) the (
m-1
)-block at row
i-1
ends.

(b)

Subcase I.B - the
m
-block which intersects with column
l_{0}
at row
i
begins after the (
m-1
)-block at row
i-1
ends.

Figure C.3
:
Possible subcases of Case I.

The situation to the right of a column
l^{\prime}
is schematically depicted in Fig.
C.4
.

Figure C.4
:
The schematic configuration to the right of the column
l^{\prime}
. The grey block is an
(m-1)
-block in Fig.
C.3
.

Since Condition 3 (row-counting) is satisfied, considering row
i-2
we may write (see Fig.
C.4
)

\sum_{j<i-1}\#(m-1)^{k}_{j}\geq\sum_{j<i-1}\#(m)^{k}_{j}\mathrm{,}

(C.4)

but

\tilde{\#}(m-1)^{k}_{l^{\prime}}=\sum_{j<i-1}\#(m-1)^{k}_{j}\mathrm{,}

(C.5)

and

\tilde{\#}(m)^{k}_{l^{\prime}}=\sum_{j<i-1}\#(m)^{k}_{j}\mathrm{.}

(C.6)

Thus, we obtain

\tilde{\#}(m-1)^{k}_{l^{\prime}}\geq\tilde{\#}(m)^{k}_{l^{\prime}}\mathrm{.}

(C.7)

Subcase I.A

Notice that the number of
(m-1)
-boxes added to the left of
l^{\prime}
is greater or equal to the corresponding number of
m
-boxes, counted up to the column
l
(see Fig.
C.3
), so from (
C.7
) we see that (
C.3
) is non-negative.

Subcase I.B

It suffices to check that (see Fig.
C.3
)

\sum_{j<i}\#(m-1)^{k}_{j}\geq\sum_{j\leq i}\#(m)^{k}_{j}\mathrm{.}

(C.8)

Just as in case of Condition 3, we have three cases depicted in Fig.
C.1
. The only difference here is that partial sums of telescopes under consideration are taken not up to the same row, but up to two consecutive rows –
(i-1)
for label
m-1
and
i
for label
m
. Hence, it is clear that (
C.3
) is satisfied.

Case II

(a)

Configuration in Case II.

(b)

Reduction to Case I.

Figure C.5
:
Reduction of Case II to Case I - the applied change of the choice of the column
l_{0}
does not decrease (
C.3
).

Note that the the column
l_{0}
can be moved to the left without decreasing (
C.3
) (see Fig.
C.5
), so that it goes through the last box of the lower
m
-block. Thus, Case II can be reduced to Case I. This ends the whole proof.
